B.4 Pseudo-variables

PL/I has the following pseudo-variables:

  • ONSOURCE
  • PAGENO
  • STRING
  • SUBSTR
  • UNSPEC

A pseudo-variable can be used, in certain assignment contexts, in place of an ordinary variable reference. For example: 


SUBSTR(S,2,1) = 'A';
This assigns the character <BIT_STRING>(A) to a 1-character substring of S, beginning at the second character of S.

A pseudo-variable can be used wherever the following three conditions are true:

  • The syntax specifies a variable reference.
  • A value is explicitly assigned to the variable.
  • The context does not require the variable to be addressable.

Pseudo-variables are used most often in the following locations:

  • The left side of an assignment statement
  • The input target of a GET statement

Note that a pseudo-variable cannot be used in preprocessor statements or in an argument list. For example: 


CALL P(SUBSTR(S,2,1));

Here, SUBSTR is interpreted as a built-in function reference, not as a pseudo-variable. The actual argument passed to procedure P is a dummy argument containing the second character of string S.

Appendix C
Implementation-defined Features

The PL/I language features listed below are termed implementation-defined : their specification is regarded as completing the definition of the language for a particular implementation. A brief description of each feature is given.

  1. The actual restrictions imposed, if any, under the provisions for restrictions on the size of constructs or in addressing.
  2. Whether or not case mapping is performed on identifiers.
  3. The mapping functions for external-names and identifiers to environment-names.
  4. Determination of the procedure to be executed initially (which must not be in a package).
  5. ENVIRONMENT attribute and option syntax and semantics.
  6. OPTIONS attribute and option syntax and semantics.
  7. Consistency requirements for ENVIRONMENT and OPTIONS attributes in EXTERNAL declarations, and for OPTIONS attributes in ENTRY references.
  8. The form of the file name in the INCLUDE construct.
  9. Interpretation of the UNSPEC pseudo-variable.
  10. Result of the UNSPEC built-in function.
  11. Length of string returned by TIME built-in function and of the DATETIME built-in function.
  12. Extralingual characters in data character set.
  13. Collating sequence, hardware representations, graphic representations, and symbol names of an implementation’s character set and the hardware representations of the control character sequences returned by the ISOCHAR built-in function.
  14. The external representations of the set of characters in which PL/I programs are written.
  15. Representation of currency symbol and digit and sign symbols.
  16. Default precisions of arithmetic data.
  17. Maximum number of digits for each combination of base and scale and elsewhere).
  18. Precision of integer type.
  19. Accuracy and range of floating-point results of expressions and built-in functions and
  20. elsewhere.
  21. Accuracy and range of ROUND built-in function with floating-point argument.
  22. Results of numeric conversions.
  23. Number of digits in the exponent of a floating-point number.
  24. Results of the MAXVAL, MINVAL, EPSILON, and RADIX arithmetic-enquiries.
  25. Result of the EXPONENT built-in function and effect of the EXPONENT pseudo-variable.
  26. Default AREA size.
  27. Size of an area-value passed as a dummy argument.
  28. Use of AREA size specification.
  29. Information output when SNAP is specified in ON statement.
  30. Form of comment output as standard system action.
  31. Value returned by ONCODE built-in function.
  32. Standard system action for STORAGE condition.
  33. Standard system action for ERROR condition.
  34. Circumstances in which TRANSMIT condition is raised.
  35. Circumstances in which KEY condition is raised.
  36. Circumstances under which records are written, or not written, when the RECORD condition is raised and normal return occurs, and the values of those records.
  37. Raising of RECORD condition by WRITE and REWRITE statements.
  38. Concrete representation of a dataset+.
  39. The “size” of a record>.
  40. Length of a key
  41. Representation of stream dataset control items.
  42. Determination of a fdataset, on file opening.
  43. Length of file title.
  44. Default LINESIZE for a STREAM OUTPUT file.
  45. Default PAGESIZE for a PRINT file.
  46. Default tab positions for a PRINT file.
  47. Position of records in a KEYED SEQUENTIAL file.
  48. Maximum number-of-digits used in editing relative to a fixed-point-format.
